          •1-1.    DESCRIPTION OF DISTRlQI..     This district is composed
     of certain, quiet, low-density residential areas of the city
     plus certain open areas where similar residential development
     appears likely to occur.          The regulations for this district
     are designed to stabilize and protect the essential characteris-
     tics of the district, to promote and encourage a suitable envir-
     onment for family life where children are members of most fami-
     lies and to prohibit all activities of a commercial nature.             To
     these ends, development is limited to a relatively low concentra-
     tion and permitted uses are limited basically to single unit
     dwellings providing homes for the residents plus certain addition·

    • l -4 ( 2 ) ( a ) •                         All structures shall be set in
 a distance of not less than twenty feet from each front line of
 the Zone Lot; provided, however, that on the two shorter dimen-
 sions of any block oblong in shape, the front setback may be re-
 duced to five feet for electric substations, gas regulator
 stations and utility pumping stations and to ten feet for all
 other structures; and provided, further, that detached accessory
 structures and accessory uses, except garages, shall be set in
 a sufficient distance from each front line of the Zone Lot so
 that such structures and uses are located only on the rear one-
 fourth of interior Zone Lots and on corner Zone Lots are located
only on the rear p2rt of the Zone Lot which is adjacent to and
corresponding with the rear one-fourth of abutting interior Zone
Lots and no closer to the side street right-of-way than one-half
the dimension of the corner Zone Lot, measured perpendicularly
from the side street right-of-way •
    • l-4(2)(b).           Rear Setback.        If no alley abuts the rear line
of the Zone Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures
shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet and all
other structures shall be set in a distance of not less than
twenty feet from each rear line of the Zone Lot.                   If an alley abuts
the rear line of the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports
opening directly on the alley shall be set in a distance of not

 less than five feet from the alley line; detached accessory
 structures {including garages and carports which do not open
 directly on the alley) and fixtures for the disposal of trash
 and garbage may be located on the alley line and all other
 structures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty
 feet from the center line of the abutting alley; provided, how-
 ever, that if a Zone Lot has more than one rear line by reason
 of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setbacks shall be required
 from only one rear line and the other rear line or lines shall,
 for the purposes of setbacks, be deemed side lines of the Zone
 Lot.       Whether or not an alley abuts the rear line of the Zone
 Lot, electric substations, gas regulator stations and utility
 pumping stations which do not have a rear entrance may be lo-
 cated on the rear line of the Zone Lot; if such structures do
 have a rear entrance they shall be set in a distance of not
 less than five feet from the rear line of the Zone Lot •
       • l-4(2)(c).   Side_Setback.          On Zone Lots less than thirty
 feet in width, all structures shall be set in a distance of
 not less than three feet from each side line of the Zone Lot.
 On Zone Lots thirty or more feet in width, all structures shall
 be set in not less than the following distances from each side
 line of the Zone Lot:          ( l)   Any structure    designed or used,
 either wholly or partly, for residential occupancy - five feet;
 (2)       Electric substation, gas regulator station - five feet;
 (3)       Any structure containing any other Use by Right - ten feet;
 (4)       Any structure containing an Accessory Use - five feet.            Any
 accessory structure which is located entirely on the rear thirty
 feet of the Zone Lot may be located within not less than two
 feet from each side line of the Zone Lot •

   • l-4(3).        Bulk of Structures.        No part of any structure (ex-
cept church spires, church towers, flagpoles, antennas, chimneys,
flues, vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up through
bulk limits which are defined by planes starting at horizontal
lines which are co-directional to the front, rear and side lines
of the Zone Lot and pass through points ten feet above the mid-
point of each such front, rear and side line and which planes
extend up over the Zone Lot at an angle of forty-five degrees
with respect to the horizontal (a pitch of one foot additional
rise for each foot additional setback).                (See Graphic Fig. No. 1)

   • 2-1.     DESCRill.IQN OF DISTRIQI.   This district is composed of
certain, quiet, low-density residential areas of the city plus
certain open areas where similar residential development appears
likely to occur,       The regulations for this district are designed
to stabilize and protect the essential characteristics of the
district, to promote and encourage a suitable environment for
family life where children are members~ of most families and to
prohibit all activities of a commercial nature except certain
enumerated Home Occupations (including room renting) controlled
by specific limitations governing the size and extent of such
non-residential activities.        To these ends, development is limi-
ted to a relatively low concentration and permitted uses are
limited basically to single unit dwellings providing homes for
the residents plus certain additional uses, such as Rchools,
parks, churches and certain public facilities, which serve the
residents of the district •
   • 2-2.    LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.    Within not
more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with
the following Limitations:

                                      • 2-4(1).     Zone Lot for Structures.      A separate ground area, here
                             in called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided and continu-
                             ously maintained for each structure containing a Use by Right.
                             Each Zone Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occu-
                             pied only by the structure containing a Use by Right and a subor-
                             dinate structure or structures containing only Accessory Uses.                   The
                             Zone Lot for each structure designed or used, either wholly or part
                             ly, for residential occupancy shall be not less than fifty feet
                             wide at the front setback line for structures, shall contain not
                             less than 6,000 square feet and shall provide not less than 3,000
                             square feet of Unobstructed Open Space, provided, however, that
                             the requirement as to minimum width may be waived on a Zone Lot
                             adjoined on both sides by Zone Lots with structures thereon or on
                             a Zone Lot adjoined on one side by a street and on the other side
                             by a Zone Lot with a structure or structures thereon.                Upon appli-

    • 2-4(2)(b).   Rear Setback.     If no alley abuts the rear line
of the Zone Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures
shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet and all
other structures shall be set in a distance of not less than
twenty feet from each rear line of the Zone Lot.            If an alley
abuts the rear line of the Zone Lot, detached garages and car-
ports opening directly on the alley shall be set in a d~tance
of not less than five feet from the alley line; detached acces-
sory structures ( including garages and carports which do not oper,
directly on the alley) and fixtures for the disposal of trash
and garbage may be located on the alley line and all other
structures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty
feet from the center line of the abutting alley; provided, how-
ever, that if a Zone Lot has more than one rear line by reason
of abutting alleys, the aforesaid setbacks shall be required from
only one rear line and the other rear line or lines shall, for
the purposes of setbacks, be deemed side lines of the Zone Lot.
Whether or not an alley abuts the rear line of the Zone Lot,
electric substations, gas regulator statiorys and utility pumping
stations which do not have a rear entrance may be located on the

    • 3-1.    DESCRIPTION_QE....QISTBJ..Q:.   This district is composed of
                .   t~

certain medium~density residential areas of the city representin~
                    e
a compatible c9-mingling of single unit and multiple unit dwell-
ings, plus certain open areas where similar residential develop-
ment appears likely to occur.          The regulations for this district
are designed to stabilize and protect the essential characteris-
tics of the district, to promote and encourage a suitable envir-
onment for family life where children are members of many fami-
lies and to prohibit all activities of a commercial nature ex-
cept certain enumerated Home Occupations (including room rent-
ing) controlled by specific limitations governing the size and
extent of such non-residential activities.          To these- ends, devel-
opment is limited to a medium concentration and permitted uees
are typically single and two-unit dwellings, courts and garden
apartments providing homes for the residents plus certain addi-
tional uses, such as schools, parks, churches and certain public
facilities, which serve the residents of the district.            However,
large apartments, with corresponding proportions of oµn space,
also may be developed under prescribed standards of density and
open space.

   • 4-1.
                              --
             DESCRI~TION OF DISTRICT • This district is composed of
             ---··
certain high-den~ity residential areas of the city plus certain
open areas where similar development appears likely to occur.
The regulations for this district are designed to &abilize and
protect the essential characteristics of the district, to promote
and encourage, insofar as compatible with the intensity of land
use, a suitable environment for family life composed predominantly
of an adult population and to prohibit all activities of a commer-
cial nature except ~ertiio.ienumerated Home Occupations (including
room renting) controlled by specific limitations ~overning the size
and extent of such non-residential activities.          To these ends,
this district is protected against encroachment of general commer-
cial or industrial uses while the regulations permit high-density
development consistent with the high concentration of persons and
land valuation.          All residential types of structures, including in-
stitutions such as hospitals and colleges, are permitted but the
predominant structural types will be apartments and multiple unit
dwellings plus certain additional uses such as schools, parks,
churches and certain public facilities which serve the residents
of the district.

   • 5-1.     DESCRIPTION OF DISTRICT.   This district is composed of
certain of the very high or highest-density residential areas of
the city, ordinarily located between residential and commercial
areas, plus certain open areas where similar development appears
likely to occur.      The regulations for this district are designed
to stabilize and protect the essential characteristics of the dis-
trict, to promote and encourage, insofar as compatible with the
high intensity of land use, a suitable environment for family life
composed almost entirely of an adult population, children are
rarely present, and to permit certain commercial uses of a charac-
ter unlikely to ·develop general concentration of traffic, crowds
of customers and generaL;outdoor advertising.      To these ends,
retail activity is sharply limited and this district is protected
against encroachment of general commercial or industrial uses
while the regulations permit high-density development consistent
with the high concentration of persons and land valuation.      All
residential types of structures, for both permanent and transient
occupancy and including institutions, are permitted plus structures
for commercial uses conforming to the pattern of the district.

any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •

   •6-1.     Q~Bl£.I.1ON·OF DISTRICT.   This district is composed of
certain land and structures used primarily to provide office space
for services related to dental and medical care and'for office
type services either for the residents of nearby residential
areas or characterized by a low volume of direct daily customer
contact~     The volume of vehicular traffic is usually low.    Usually
this district is located away from main arterial highways, charac-
teristically is small in size and is situated near major hospital~
between large business ~reas and residential areas or entirely
surrounded by residential districts.      The regulations are designed
to permit     development of the enumerated functions, limited by
                                                                    '

standards designed to protect the abutting or surrounding residen-
tial districts.      To these ends, the regulations establish stand-
ards comparable to the standards for low-density residential
districts, resulting in similar building bulk and re~aining the low
concentration of pedestrian and vehicular traffic.

    •7-12. B-2 DISTRICTS PREVIOUSLY ESTABLISHED •
    • 7-1.     DESCRIPTION OF CTSTR,.Ig:.   This district is composed of
certain land and structures used primarily to provide the retail-
ing of commodities classed by merchants as "convenience goods",
such as groceries and drugs, and the furnishing of certain per-
sonal services, such as the cutting of hair and repairing of
shoes, satisfying the daily and weekly household or personal
needs of the residents of abutting residential neighborhoods.
Purchasing in the district is primarily by women, frequently
accompanied by their children, and the volume of pedestrian
traffic in proportion to automobile traffic entering the district
is much higher than in other retail Business districts.         This dis-
trict is located either on or away from main arterial highways,

                                    -69-

=== page 70 ===
     60       70




c~aracteristically is small, alm~t always is entirely surround-
ed by residential districts ahd is located at a convenient
walking distance from the edges of the surrounding residential
districts it is designed to serve.             The regulations are.designed
to permit development of the enumerated functions, limited by
standards designed to protect t'~e abutting or sorrounding resi-
dential districts.         To these ends, the regulations establish
standards comparable to the standards for low-density residential
districts, resulting in similar building bulk and retaining the
relatively low concentration of vehicular traffic as compared tb
other retail business districts.            Of great importance is the nec-
essity that the areas selected for this district actually be
developed as permitted.          Otherwise, the surrounding res1dential
areas will lack the retail services needed for stable neighbor-
hoods.      To insure the provision of essential retail services to
a neighborhood, the privilege of providing these services is
matched by the responsibility and duty to so provide.              Consequent-
ly, the right to develop this district may be lost if actual de-
velopment does not occur •
   • 7-2.    IJMITATIONS Of'.'.,f_~TERNAL_gfFECTS OF USES.      Within not
more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with
the following Limitations:
   .7-2(1).        Encl.Q.§_ure of_JJses. Every use, unless expressly ex-
empted by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety with-
in a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from
the.requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase
"need not be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted •
   •7-2(2).        VDl.!:dl!l~_of Sound Generated.   Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the vol-
ume of sound inherently and recurrently .generated does not ex-
ceed sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone
Lot on which the use is located.

    .7-3(3).    Accessory Uses.   Incidental only to a Use by Right,
any use which complies with all of the following conditions may be
operated, either in a detached structure or in the structure con-
taining the Use by Right, as an Accessory Use:
    .7-3(3)(a).     Is clearly incidental and customary to and commonly
associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
    • 7-3(3) (b).   Is operated and maintained under the same ownership
and on the same Zone Lot as the Use by Right •
    • 7-3(3){c).    Does not include stroctures or structural features
inconsistent with the Use by Right;
    .7-3(3)(d).     Does not include residential occupancy except by
employees employed on the premises a:1d the immediate . families of
such employees;
    .7-3{3)(e).     Does not utilize a gross floor area in excess of
ten per cent of the gross floor area utilized by the Use by Right;
provided, however,
    .7-3{3)(f).     That the gross floor area utilized by all Accessory
Uses (except garages, loading docks and company dining rooms) to all
Uses by Right in the same structure shall not be in excess of ten
per cent of the gross floor area utilized by all of the Uses by
Right •

only by the structure containing a Use by Right and one subordinate
structure containing only Accessory Uses.         Upon application to and

and areaof a designated Zone Lot may be amended if full compliance
with all requirements of this ordinance can be maintained •

              land area included in the district, the ·owner or owners of all
               structures then existing thereon and all incumbrancers of both
              said land area and structures, hereinafter called the agiicants,
              accompanied by sufficient evidence to establish that the applicants
              are the owners and all the incumbrancers of the designated land

              Planned Building Groups and if so based shall comply with all re-
              quirements of that Article.           Upon, but not before, the approval,
              registration and recording of the Development Plan as hereinafter
              set forth, the applicants for such Plan shall be entitled to apply
            for such permits and certificates as are made necessary by such Plan.

   • 7-10(1)(£).   Effect of Registration and Recording.   All Devel-
opment Plans registered and recorded hereunder shall be binding
upon the applicants therefor, their successors and assigns, shall
limit and control the issuance and validity of all zoning permits
and zoning certificates and shall restrict and limit the construc-
tion, location, use and operation of all land and structures in-
cluded within such Plans to all conditions and limitations set
forth in such Plans; provided, however, that upon application to
and approval by the Department of Zoning Administration, based only
upon a showing of engineering necessity therefor, minor changes in
the location of structures may be permitted if such minor changes
will not cause any of the following circumstances to occur:
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-l).    A change in the character of the development;
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-2).    An increase in the ratio of the gross floor
areas in structures to the area of any Zone Lot;

tions between buildings;
   .7-lO(l){f)(f-5).    An increase in the problems of circulation,
safety and utilities;
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-6).    An increase of the external effects on adja-
cent property;
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-7).    A reduction in the originally approved set-
backs from property lines;
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-8).    An increase in ground coverage by structures;
   .7-lO(l)(f)(f-9).    A reduction in the ratio of off-street parking
and loading space to gross floor area in structures;
   .7-lO(l}{f)(f-10).   A change in the subject, size, lighting,
flashing, animation or orientation of originally approved signs.

      • 8-12. B-3 DISTRICTS PREVIOUSLY ESTABLISHED •
      •8-1.    DESCRIPTI~~f_DISIRICT.      This district is composed of
certain land and structures used primarily to provide the retailing
of commodities classed by merchants as "convenience goods", such as
groceries and drugs, "durable goods", such as household appliances
and home furnishings, "fashion goods", such as clothing and new
automobiles, and the furnishing of certain personal services, such
as cutting of hair and repairing of shoes, and of other major per-
sonal services, such as banks and theaters, satisfying all household
and personal needs of the residents of abutting large residential
communities.        Purchasing in the district is primarily by adults of
both sexes, sometimes accompanied by their children, and the volume
of pedestrian traffic in proportion to automobile traffic entering


                                    -86-

=== page 87 ===
                                                                             50      87


the district is relatively low.               This district is usually located
on a major arterial highway at or near the intersection with one or
more other major arterial highways so that it is accessible from all
directions, characteristically is large, almost always is entirely
surrounded by Residential or B-1 Districts and is located at a
convenisnt driving distance from the edges of the surrounding re-
sidential districts it is designed to serve.                   The regulations are
designed to permit development of the enumerated functions, limited
by standards designed to protect the abutting or surrounding resid-
ential districts.        To these ends,· the regulations establish stan-
dards comparable to the standards for low density residential dis-
tricts, resulting in similar building bulk and retaining as low a
poncentra~ion of vehicular traffic as is compatible with the func-·
tioning of the district.             Of great importance is the necessity that
the areas selected for this district actually be developed as per-
mitted.     Otherwise, the surrounding residential areas will lack the
major retail services needed for stable communities.                   To insure the
provision of essential major retail services to a community, the pri-
vilege of providing theee services is matched by the responsibility
and duty to so provide.           Consequently, the right to develop this
district may be lost if actual development does not occur •

        • 9-1.    Q§CRitlIQN....QF orsrRICT.   This district is composed of
          -
     certain land andstructures used primarily to provide -retailing and
     personal services of all kinds, including temporary housing, for
     non-residents of the district and transients using the main arter-
     ial highways along which the district is located, to provide busi-
     ness services, such as warehousing and business machine supply
     companies, in support of the primary activities in the central
     business district and to provide locations for certain limited in-
     dustrial activities which require a higher degree of protection
     from external effects of uses than is afforded in the principal in-
     dustrial districts.      As a secondary function, retailing and person-
     al services may be provided to residents of near-by areas.          Purcha-
     sing in the district is primarily by adults of both sexes, rarely
     accompanied by their children, and the volume of pedestrian traffic
     in proportion to automobile traffic is re~atively low.         This dist-
     rict is usually located in long narrow strips paralleling arterial
     highways in the city or in areas adjacent to the central business
     district, characteristically is large, and almost always extensive-
     ly abuts residential districts.       The regulations are designed to
     permit development of the enumerated functions, limited by stand-
     ards designed to protect the abutting or surrounding residential
     districts.     T~ these ends, the regulations establish standards re-
     taining as low an intensity of use and concentration of vehicles
     as is compatible with the functioning of the district •

       • 10-1.    DESCRIITJ.QN .OFDISTRICT. This district is composed of
certain land and structures used primarily to provide retailing and
personal and business services of all kinds satisfying the needs
of transients generally and of the residents, business and industry
of the entire metropolitan area and the outlying trade area, plus
wholesaling and limited manufacturing in support of the primary
 functions.         Purchasing in the district is primarily by adults of
both sexes, ·sometimes accompanied by thei:r children,· and the volume
of pedestrian traffic in proportion to the volume of automobile and
public transit traffic entering the district is very low.           This dis-
trict is located at the area of convergence of the main arterial high-
ways and transit lines, is large, is surrounded by non-residential
districts or by the R-4 District and is centrally located with res-
pect to the entire metropolitan area.         The regulations are designed
to permit a highly concentrated, intensive development of the enumer-
ated facilities, limited by standards designed to provide light and
air for street exposures of buildings in the district and to protect
the district itself from over-intensive development of. land coverage
and over-congestion as related to the ultimate capacity of common
public facilit.i~s whi_r.h ser-..ie i.he ~ntire distrJ<.;t.   Because- transit

     10-2.   LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.          Within not
more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with
the following Limitations:
     10-2(1).     Enclosure of Uses.      ~very use, unless expressly
exempted by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety
within a completely enclosed structure; the exemption ofa use
from the requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the
phrase "need not be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted •
     • 10-2(2).   Voluffi§
                        .fl\.
                               of Sound Generated.
                              ..,
                                                     Every use, unless ex-
pressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that
                          )'


the volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does
not exceed seventy decibels at any point of any boundary line
of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
     • 10-2(3).   Vibration Generated.        Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated
is not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any
boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
     • 10-2(4).   Emission of Heat, Glare, Radiation and Fume§.         Every
use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any
boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
     • 10-2(5).   Outdoor Storage and Waste Disposal •
     • 10-2(5)(a).       No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids
or gases shalr be stored in bulk above ground.            Tanks or drums
of fuel directly connecting with heating devices or appliances
located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or drums of fuel are
excluded from this provision;
     .10-2(5)(b).        All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw
materials and products shall be enclosed by a fence or wall
adequate to conceal such facilities from adjacent property;

 stored outdoors only in closed containers •

     • 10-4.   E.fillMITTED STRUCTURES •
     • 10-4(1).    Zone Lot for Structu~es. A ground area, herein call-
 ed the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided and continuously
 maintained for all structures containing a Use by Right.           Each Zone
 Lot shall have at least one front line and shall be occupied only~
 one or more structures each containing a Use by Right and an equal
 number of subordinate structures containing only Accessory Uses.
 Upon application to and approval by the Department of Zoning Admini
 stration, the boundaries and area of a designated Zone Lot may be

 amended if full compliance with all requirements of this ordinance
 can be maintained •
     • 19-4(2).   Bulk of Structures.        No part of any structure or
 fixture thereof shall extend out over p.1blic rights-of~way and no
part of any structure (except church spires, church towers, flag-
poles, antennas, chimneys, flues, vents or accessory wat~r tanks.).,

shall project up through bulk limits which are defined by planes
starting at the center lines of all streets within and abutting
the district and extending u~ over the lone Lot at an angle of 75
degrees and 4 minutes with respect to the horizontal (a pitch of
3,75 feet additional rise for each foot additional setback).
(See Graphic Fig. No. 3)
   .10-4(3).   Maximum Gross Floor Area in Structures.   The sum
total of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot,
excluding garage space, shall not be greater than ten times the
area of the Zone Lot on which the structures are located,
   .10-4(4).   Outside hrea of Window Exposure.   Each legally re-
quired window shal~ have not less than the following amount of
outside exposure d~termined in the following manner:     From a re-
ference point located at the bottom center of the window, extend
outward at right angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector
of 140 degrees, centered on the window, with a radius of ten feet.
Within this sector the minimum required outside area of exposure
for the window shall be any open sector or combination of sectors
totalling 70 degrees.   Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone
Lots, the area which may be credited as outside area of window
exposure, extends to required setback lines, regardless of the
actual location of structures.    (See Graphic Fig. No. 5)
   .10-5.   PERMITTED SIGNS.   The provisions of Article 613.    Permit-
ted Signs shall be of no force and effect in this district;
provided, however, that no sign, outdoor commercial advertising
device or lighting device constituting a nuisance to an adjacent
residential district, because of lighting glare, focus, animation
or flashing of the sign, lighting or advertising device, shall be
erected or continued in operation •
   •10-6.   OFF-STREET_EARKING REQUIREMENT~ The provisions of
Article 614.   Off-Street Parking Requirements shall be of no force
and effect in this district.

        • 11-1.    DESQRIPTION QI_QISTRfil.
                         :   r,
                                                 This district is composed of
     certain lands located between general industrial uses and quiet
     residential areas and of certain other lands so located that resi-
     dential development is not feasible but where residential develop-
     ment is in close proximity.       The regulations of this district are
     int~nded to provide structural standardsr standards of intensity
     of use and standards of external effects compatible with the sur-
     rounding or abutting residential districts.         To these ends, devel-
     opment is limited to a low concentration, external effects are
     limited and permitted uses are limited to those manufacturing and
     wholesaling activities which can be operated in a clean and quiet
     manner, plus certain public facilities which are needed:to serve
     the occupants of the district and residents of adjoining residen-
 tial districts •
        • 11-2.    LIMITATION§.__Qt] EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES.   Within not
     more than three years from the date onvhich the following pro-

     . 12-1.    DE.SCRIPTION OF DISTf.UCT.          This district is composed of
 certain lands so situated as to be suitable for industrial develop-
 ment, but where the modes of operations of the industry will direc-
 tly affect nearby residential and business uses.               The purpose of
 the district is to permit the normal operations of almost all in-
 dustries, subject only to those regulations needed to control con-
 gestion and to protect nearby residential and business districts •
     • 12-2.    LIMITATIONS ON EXT!;RN61,_.EFFECT§ OF USES. Within not
 more than three years from the date on which the following pro-
 visions become effective, every use shall be made to comply with
 the following Limitations:
     .12-2(1).      iplume of Sound Generated.          Every use, unless expres~
 ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volumt
 of sound inherently and recurren~ly generated does not exceed seve~
 ty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on
 which the use,is located •
     •12-2(2).     Vibration Generated.           Every use shall be so operated
 that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
 not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
 line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

boundary line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
   • 12-2(4).   Q!J!doo1:_ Store,gi,_and Was~ Disposal.

   .12-2(4)(b).     All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw ma-

        •15-1.       ~fil.EIIQ!':LOF DISTRICT •. This district is composed of
     certain lands used for off-street parking where they abut a busi-
     ness or industrial district, and are surrounded on at least two
     sides by residence areas.           The district regulations are designed
 to permit off-street parking facilities to be established to sup-
 port business an.d industrial uses, without permitting the business
     or industrial uses per se to extend into residential areas, and to
 reduce congestion in the streets caused by certain uses with a mini-
 mum of incompatibility with abutting residential areas.               To these
     ends, the uses·are limited solely to parking and a continuation of
 existing railroad rights-cf-way •
        • 15-2..     PERMITTED USES.      No land shall be used or occupied and
 no structure shall be designed, erected, altered, used or occupied
 except for one only of the following Uses by Right •
        •15-2(1).       Uses by Rigbt.     The following uses may be operated
 as Uses by Right:
        .15-2(2)(a).       Parking lot:     A parking lot operated and maintain-
 ed in accordance with all of the following conditions:
        .15-2(l)(a){a-l).           Is not used for the sale, repair or dismant-
 ling of any vehicles, equipment, materials or supplies;
        .15-2(2)(a)(a-2).       Is properly graded for drainage; surfaced
 with concrete, asphaltic concrete, asphalt, oil or any other dust-
 fr~e surfacing and maintained in good condition, free of weeds, ·
 dust, trash or debris;

   .15-2(l)(a)(a-3).    Is provided with entrances and exits so lo-

   .15-2(l)(a)(a-4).    Is provided with wheel or bumper guards
so located and arranged that no part of any parked vehicle will
extend beyond the boundaries of the parking lot;
   .15-2(l)(a)(a-5).    Lighting facilities are so arranged that
they neither unreasonably disturb occupants of adjacent residen-
tial properties nor interfere with traffic;
   .15-2(l)(a)(a-6).    Has a fence on each boundary of the parking
lot which abuts a residential district; the fence is between three
and five feet high at the grade of the parking lot at the fence
line and is ~esigned to obscure from abutting residential districts
the direct light from automobile headlights; the fence is designed
to be, and can and will be, maintained in a sightly condition and
will not be used as a support for any signs except those signs
permitted in the district;
   .15-2(l)(a)(a-7).    Has only one attendant shelter building con-
taining not more than fifty square feet of gross floor area and
set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from any boundary
of the parking lot which abuts a residential district •

and which provides entrances and exits so located as to minimize
traffic congestion and the effect of headlights at night;

cluding railway yards, maintenance or fueling facilities •

        ;18-9.     TIJ\,1E LIMITAJJONS ON CONSTRUQIION.   All improvements
     indicated on the Site Plan adopted and approved, incorporated here-
     in and made a part hereof, shall be commenced not later than June
     1, 1956, shall be prosecuted with due diligence and shall be com-

 pleted not later than June 1, 1958, provided, however, that if
 such improvements are not constructed within the aforesaid time
no building permit shall be issued for the construction of any
 structures within this district until such district has been zoned
 to and for its most appropriate use.
